- **The agents pane in the session's view.** The `agents` pane kind draws the session's dispatched agents, as wide as its widest row reads at the current text size and not resizable, with a header carrying the title, its sub-line, `All`, a divider, previous, next, `Open in new window` and close, a child's view reading the breadcrumb `Sidekicks › <name>` with `‹` and `›` stepping between siblings and `All` returning to the list; like every side pane it can move into a window of its own (§Detached panes). One list view and one child view switch in place. **The list view** carries a bar with `Stop all running` while anything is live, which asks once naming how many it stops, and the finished-rows toggle, off by default, reading its value as a word — `Hide finished` or `Show finished` — and never carrying a pressed tint, over one tree of every agent in the order the work happened, finished rows dimmed; a row is a button in the tab order, opening its child on a click, Enter or Space, while a press on a button or inside a confirm within the row does not open it; a child that raises an approval flashes its row for 900 ms to say where the approval came from; a row carries the agent's swatch — a saved definition's own icon, or the robot glyph where it sets none, on the agent's hue, never an initial letter or a photo — its name, model and reasoning effort on one line — `<name> · <model> · <effort>`, the effort one word — `via <agent>` naming the agent that was asked where it was a peer call, as plain muted text after the model and effort and never a pill, its act line in its own state color, its result once finished — a child that failed reading `✗ failed · <reason>` in red, the only mark a child row takes — an amber waiting control while it waits on a person, and a foot with tools, tokens, spend and duration. Depth indents by one step of the current text size per level and draws a hairline connector to the row's left. **The child view** holds the child's head — its swatch, its name, the one-line ask clamped to one line with the whole ask in its hover title, and its meta line carrying model, reasoning effort as one word, `via <agent>` where it was a peer call in the same plain muted text, tokens, spend, start, and the chain walking every ancestor as pressable links — then that child's own flow in the transcript's own rendering at pane scale, with the pane's foot holding the child's own working line, any approval card inline, and the steer box with its send. A live child view streams only while it is open. Where its rows would be, a child's view says which fact holds rather than drawing an empty body: `No output yet.` while the child has produced nothing, `Loading this sidekick's work…` while its rows are fetched, and, in one red line, `This sidekick's record could not be read · <cause>`; a child whose dispatcher is missing from the record carries the red line `This sidekick's dispatcher is missing from the record` above its row and draws at the root of the tree. Its controls are words at the end of that view's own working line rather than buttons on a card: pause and interrupt, on the same terms as the session's, neither drawn once the child is done while the steer box stays, so a send starts the child again; a child stopped for good draws that word and no box. Interrupt is the last word in the line, so every interrupt on the screen sits in one place. The pane carries no pause or stop control on a card, no resume word, no kind label, no relay note, no mechanism tag, no overflow menu, no open control, no steer button, no second approval control and no dependency graph.
- Owning plan: Plan-013
- Note: this view binds to the Spec-014 surface as registered in orchestration-payloads.md §Plan-013.

### Provider Accounts And Cost View (→ Plan-023 Provider Accounts And Credential Homes)

- Data sources: the node-local `providerAccount` wire namespace through `bridge.daemon.call(…)` for the registry read, current-account selection, and per-account authentication probe result, and the session cost receipt read surface for spend — concrete method names for both are registered in api-payload-contracts.md per [Spec-025 §Interfaces And Contracts](025-provider-accounts-and-credential-homes.md#interfaces-and-contracts) and [Spec-014 §Session Cost Receipt](014-multi-agent-orchestration.md#session-cost-receipt)
- Renders: a **provider page of one section per provider**, each section in two halves — that provider's own settings above the accounts registered against it. **The provider's half** carries: the provider's own line, reading as installed and signed in with its version, as not installed, or as undecided where the check could not settle, and never as a guess — a not-installed reading carrying `Install` beside it on every platform, which runs that provider's own documented installer once, as the person, where the service runs — with no administrator step, no terminal and no input, `Installing Claude Code…` and `Cancel` while it runs, no time limit of the app's own, the installer's own timeouts bounding it, and the provider checked again afterwards — while a failure reads `Claude Code did not install: <the installer's own reason>` with the command under it as text to run by hand; the path to that provider's command in one field, reading as auto-detected while it is empty, re-checked where it now points whenever it changes and re-reading that provider's model catalog with it so the model control never offers a model the new command does not have, and refusing in place where nothing runnable sits there; a switch that makes the provider available for new sessions, which every provider can turn off, and which is grayed with its reason where that provider's command is not installed; a bound on how many helper processes the provider may run at one time, empty by default and reading `Unlimited`, which is no limit, the daemon lifting Codex's own built-in limit; `0` running none, the daemon withholding the provider's helper tool; and any other number that many, passed to Codex as its own limit, one more than the person's number because Codex counts the lead, and held on Claude Code, which has no setting for it, by the daemon's `PreToolUse` hook on the Agent tool, which holds a new helper's start until one of that session's running helpers finishes, with no ceiling of the console's own — how deep an agent's own helpers may go is bounded neither by this control nor anywhere else in the console; the standing rules that provider holds on this machine, read from the provider's own files in every account home and every attached project, one row per rule reading what it allows or refuses with its scope — an account home or a project — and the file it came from, and a revoke beside it that removes the rule where the provider keeps it and confirms in place, the provider following the change as it does on its own, so a loaded Codex conversation keeps the rules it loaded with (`provider.standingRuleList`, `provider.standingRuleRevoke`), a provider holding none saying so rather than drawing an empty list; the protected paths, a read-only list of the paths an agent is refused on this provider — the built-in credential files together with the deny rules the person already keeps in that provider's own configuration, global and project — each entry reading where it came from, `~/.aws/credentials · built in` or `secrets/** · Claude Code project settings`, with nothing on it to edit, the provider's own precedence deciding where two entries overlap, and each entry enforced by the provider's own rule at the levels that provider enforces it, which [Spec-010 §Required Behavior](./010-approvals-permissions-and-trust-boundaries.md#required-behavior) states for each provider; the provider-level automatic-compaction bound, which moves in steps of five between the lowest bottom stop and the highest top stop that provider's models report, so each provider's range is its own, and is mapped onto that provider's own control, drawn in both providers' sections because both expose it — a different control from a session's own bound, which sits behind that session's context ring ([Spec-011 §Context Window and Compaction](011-transcript-and-reasoning.md#context-window-and-compaction)), so the row's own line says the bound is mapped onto the provider's and that the session's stays where it is; Claude Code's output style, one choice reading the style the installed build is on with that build's other styles beside it, one line describing the style it is on and one saying the choice applies to sessions started later, no style typed and no such row under Codex; Claude Code's `Advisor`, `Off` and then the advisor models Claude Code offers, under the line `Applies to sessions started later. A session already running changes its own advisor from its composer.`, kept in the machine's settings file through `machineSettings.write` and never in Claude Code's; `Use Sidekicks in terminal Claude Code`, off and drawn under Claude Code only, which installs the app's plugin and one setting in the person's own Claude Code settings and, turned off, removes only those; `Reach Codex sessions started in a terminal`, one switch that is **on by default** and is drawn under Codex only, never under Claude Code, because the mechanism is that provider's alone — under the one line `Runs one Codex service while it is on, so sidekicks can message a Codex session you typed in a terminal.` — such a session has no row in the sessions list, and the agents reach it through the session-messaging tools — and grayed where Codex is not installed, saying `Codex is not installed.` on the row and in its own title the way the availability switch does ([Spec-014 §Sessions Talking To Each Other](./014-multi-agent-orchestration.md#sessions-talking-to-each-other)); and one action that imports that provider's own existing conversations, reporting into one progress row on the page — no pop-up, no wizard, no screen of its own — carrying a stop while it runs, skipping every session this console already holds and saying how many it skipped, counting the files it could not read and unfolding their paths on a press, saying which reason it is where there was nothing to bring in, and confirming in place before it runs where it would attach projects this console does not have — reading that provider's own folders at the press and keeping nothing between presses, so a session finished a moment ago is in the read and there is no list to go stale and no rescan to offer, and what it brings in is a copy taken when it ran, nothing re-reading a source afterwards and nothing merging what the provider added since, so importing again is the way to catch up. **The accounts half** opens with the line `New sessions run on the account marked Default. A sidekick or a workflow step can be set to a specific account instead.`; then it draws one row per account, and `Set as default` on a row says in its own line that new sessions and every running session on that provider without an account of its own follow the mark. **An account is named only by the identity its provider reports at sign-in** — the email address the provider returns, then the plan in the provider's own word, then the organization where the plan carries one — with no invented name and no typed label, because one address can hold two accounts on two plans. The one exception is an account added by a pasted token or an API key, which the provider names nowhere: it reads the name the person gave it in the paste form beside the credential's kind, `Work · Codex API key`, a name that is required, differs from that provider's other account names, and is renamed in place. Beside the identity a row carries its billing-mode chip, the `Default` mark on the one account per provider that holds it, its state in words with when it was last checked, an account whose login is gone reading `Login expired · Sign in again`, never collapsed into `Cannot tell right now`, the one remedy that applies to that state and no other, the ages the background observation writes — last checked, last refreshed, and the re-login horizon rendered as an approximation, a value the daemon reports as unknown **reading as not known rather than being fabricated** or read as though the account were fine, and ages, never countdowns, the one exception being `Login expires in about N days`, from seven days out and in the warning color from three, a token account carrying no refresh age and a Codex row no horizon — a freshness switch that is on by default and turns that observation off for this account alone without signing it out, and its quota windows per `(account, limit)` under the heading `Usage`, each labeled with its own limit rather than merged and named from the length the provider reported rather than from its place in the reply, each a bar with its figure and its reset time in words beside it, **each carrying the time it was read** as `read 7:24 PM` — a figure that moves at the end of every turn on that account, so an account being worked on reads current and the background cadence only carries the ones nothing is running on — a window that has reset reading as reset, a reading taken before that account's credential changed reading as being from before the change, an account with no reading yet reading `Not read yet` with no bar at all, and an account whose figures were taken from its own last turn rather than a read of its own — a Codex account between reads, or a Claude Code account whose read the endpoint is refusing — reading `from its last turn · <time>` instead of moving a bar; after the second refused read in a row the account's state reads `Cannot tell right now`. Under the windows sits that account's lifetime spend carried with its billing mode, so a plan seat's usage never reads as billed dollars and an account whose billing mode nobody has declared reads as not set rather than being folded unlabeled into a currency total, and beside it the account's tokens for today and this week, opening on a press to the same figures by day and by model; then the same figures summed across that provider's accounts, which state when they span more than one billing mode ([Spec-025 §Billing mode](025-provider-accounts-and-credential-homes.md#billing-mode)) and carry no other label. Every dollar figure is the cost with no mark — a provider that reports no cost of its own is priced from the daemon's own price table — `All accounts · today <n> tokens · <cost>`. Beside that line stands the tightest window any of that provider's accounts is against, naming the account, its plan and that one limit's figure, `Tightest · <email> · Max · 5-hour limit 67%`; a percentage is never summed across accounts, because a limit belongs to one account. The provider's own figures on this page are its usage windows alone: every token and dollar figure beside them is the daemon's own, and no provider report of totals, streaks or peak days is drawn. **Never rendered here:** credential-home contents, which the renderer neither reads nor receives, and a token value, which no reply carries. The list draws its loading, empty and failure states rather than an empty list — ghost rows in the shape of the real rows while it is being read, a provider with no accounts naming the way to get one, a failure carrying try-again as one faint word, a provider whose accounts carry no `Default` mark saying that new work on it will not start until one is marked, an account whose usage cannot be read drawing one quiet line in place of its meters rather than an empty bar, and a warning the daemon reports about one provider drawn as its own strip in that provider's section. Spend and tokens by day and by model are drawn here and nowhere else: no usage screen stands beside this page, and no session surface carries an account's windows or an account-wide figure.
- Interactions: register an account, remove one, make one the provider's current account, correct its billing mode, start a brokered sign-in, cancel an in-flight sign-in, supply a non-interactive token under the name the person gives it, rename such an account in place, turn the background observation off for one account or back on, turn that account's window starting off or back on under it, check one account now, reset a credential home, and run the one-time copy of the person's own provider memories into an account's own home — each forwarded to the daemon registry as one wire mutation per explicit user action, and each act on the provider's half the same: `Install` and its `Cancel`, the command path, the availability switch, the helper bound, a standing rule's revoke, the compaction bound, the output style, the advisor, the terminal plugin switch and the conversation import — with the protected-paths list the one thing on that half carrying no act at all, since nothing on it is added, edited or removed here. **Signing out and removing are two acts and never one:** signing out ends that account's login and leaves the row exactly where it is, so the same account signs in again without being registered twice, while removing forgets the registry row itself, **runs no sign-out of the provider's own**, leaves the credential folder on the machine untouched because that folder is the person's own material, confirms first because the account's spend and history go with the row, and is refused in place while a run bound to that account is live, naming the sessions. Checking one account checks that account alone — at once where its last read is at least 60 seconds old, and otherwise answering with that last read — and settles in place with one line saying what it did and did not do, since the observation reads the provider's own limits inside that account's own folder and spends no model turn, except on a pasted Claude Code token, whose limits are read from a turn of its own, so its check sends that same small priced turn: the daemon signs nothing in, writes no credential, reads one only where [ADR-026 §Decision](../decisions/026-provider-credential-custody-posture.md#decision) names it, speaks no token endpoint, and lets the provider keep its own login current. There is no credential-refresh control at all: where the shared Codex service asks the daemon for a refresh, the daemon answers it by running `account/read {refreshToken: true}` in that account's own home ([ADR-026 §Decision](../decisions/026-provider-credential-custody-posture.md#decision)), and every renewal shows on screen only as the last-refreshed age moving. What the windows carry beside them is a read again, which takes a fresh reading and moves no login of its own. **Keeping an account fresh carries one switch indented under it, and that one does spend a turn.** The nested switch reads `Start each five-hour window as soon as it opens` under Claude Code and `Start each usage window as soon as it opens` under Codex, is on by default, is off whenever its parent is off, and carries one line saying what it costs: under Claude Code `When the five-hour window resets, one short message on the smallest model starts the next one right away, so its clock runs from now. The weekly window resets at a fixed time of its own. About one small turn per window, on this account.`, and under Codex `When a window resets, one short message on the smallest model starts the new window right away, so its clock runs from now. About one small turn per window, on this account.` It draws nothing else — no row, no count and no list of the turns it spent; the turn it spends appears only in that account's tokens for the day and the week and, where the provider reports a cost, in its spend, and the limit row it belongs to carries the new window's reset time at the next read ([Spec-025 §Credential-home health observation](025-provider-accounts-and-credential-homes.md#credential-home-health-observation)). A brokered sign-in renders the provider's verification URL and, on a device-code arm, its user code with how long it has left and then, once it has run out, `Code expired · Sign in again`, the URL always drawn and selectable so a browser that did not open is not a dead end, with cancel available while the attempt is in flight; **completion arrives on the registry subscription and is never inferred from a timer**, a completion reported successful updates the row only after the daemon's own observation lands ([Spec-025 §Brokered interactive sign-in](025-provider-accounts-and-credential-homes.md#brokered-interactive-sign-in)), and a sign-in that does not finish says so where it was started, leaves the account as it was, and keeps the control that starts it again. The token field is **write-only in the renderer as well as on the wire**: masked on entry, never read back, never held in serializable renderer state a devtools inspection or crash report would capture, and cleared on submit — the wire rule protects the transport, this protects the screen. A token the provider does not accept is refused under the field it was pasted into with the field left empty and the controls still there to try another; re-supplying one targets the account already there and keeps its identity, its spend and its history; and a token account's own remedy is to mint a fresh token and paste it rather than to retry, because it carries no refresh. **Pressing an account is the account switch.** A press on the row, its `Set as default`, the `Cycle accounts` key and `/account <name>` typed in a session press the same switch, and nothing else moves the mark. It makes that account the provider's current one, moves which account new sessions run on, and moves every running session on that provider that is not pinned to one — each at its next request, a running tool finishing and never repeated, nothing interrupted and no waiting words on its working line — and it rewrites no credential, each account keeping its own home. Each moved session's transcript carries one faint collapsed row at that point, `Switched all sessions to <name>` (`<name>` being the account as the Providers page lists it), expanding to the account it came from, the account it went to and the time ([Spec-025 §Moving a session to another account](025-provider-accounts-and-credential-homes.md#moving-a-session-to-another-account)). A key cycles a provider's accounts while it is held, a small strip showing them with the mark moving on each repeat, and **the account under the mark when the key is released** becomes current, so one gesture is one switch. The session inspector's account fact reads which account a session is on and offers no switch, and this page sends no per-run account override. A press naming an account that cannot be realized is refused in place with that account's own remedy, and nothing moves. One reading on this page is its own rather than the daemon's: a provider that exposes no provider-level setting at all reads that the provider decides it itself — the form that section takes, drawn only where that is true, which is neither provider today because both expose the compaction bound
- State handling: every rendered figure is wire-verbatim — no client-side derivation, re-summing, or parallel per-account tally. An account's lifetime spend and the total across accounts are each read as the daemon serves them from its committed-spend fold, one figure per account and one for the set, and the renderer assembles neither of them from the other and neither from per-session receipts, which is the client-side re-aggregation [Spec-014 §Cost Figure Display Consistency](014-multi-agent-orchestration.md#cost-figure-display-consistency) forbids. Spend and tokens by day and by model are served the same way, as group-bys the daemon performs over its own per-turn usage rows ([Spec-025 §State And Data Implications](025-provider-accounts-and-credential-homes.md#state-and-data-implications)); the renderer slices nothing itself. Account-scoped state (authentication probe result, provider quota) is held per account rather than once per provider — and quota per `(account, limit)` rather than per account, since one account may stand against several limits at once — and a reading observed under a superseded credential generation is re-read rather than rendered as current. **The page derives no eligibility of its own**: health, readiness, expiry, and which controls are available are all rendered from what the daemon reports and disabled where it reports nothing, the fail-closed-projection discipline `I-002-18` states for the edit affordance. A page that recomputed any of them would be the surface nothing enforces
- Owning plan: Plan-023
- Note: a settings page, not a V1 Signature Feature — it composes through the same preload bridge under the same read-and-steer rule.

### MCP Servers View — the settings-level governance page (→ Plan-022 MCP Server Configuration And Governance)

- Data sources: the node-local `mcp.*` wire namespace through `bridge.daemon.call(…)` for the server inventory and per-server detail, and `mcp.subscribe` — the live governance stream — as the update channel, so the page refreshes on the daemon's own transitions rather than by polling, which [Spec-024 §Status Observation and Events](024-mcp-server-configuration-and-governance.md#status-observation-and-events) forbids the daemon to do on the providers and which this page must not reintroduce from above. The page subscribes to that stream **before** it reads the inventory, so nothing that changes between the two is lost, and status arriving on it is applied to the row it names rather than triggering a whole-inventory re-read. Plan-022 already registers every operation this page calls; this view mints none — no operation, no event kind and no error — so a control that would need a new one is not a control this page draws
- Renders: a settings-level page in two panes — the server list on the left, the selected server's detail on the right. Each list row carries the server name, its provider and configuration scope, the normalized connection status, and an enabled marker. **The connection status is one of five words** — connected, starting, needs sign-in, failed, and unknown — and this page is where they are stated: every other surface in the console that says what a tool server is doing, the composer's tool-servers chip among them, draws one of those same five from this one inventory. A server name held in two places is shown as two rows rather than resolved away, the copy a project does not use naming the one that takes its place. With nothing selected the detail pane says so in one line and asks for a pick. The list draws its first read rather than an empty list: `Reading the server list…` while it is read, `The server list could not be read.` with `Try again` as one faint word at the end of its line, and a provider with no tool servers set up saying so. The detail pane adds the server's tool inventory — per tool, whether it is on, whether it asks before running, and what happens if a call is interrupted, each carrying its effective value beside whether that value is the server's own or set here — the effective idempotency class among them. Configuration content splits three ways on this page rather than being uniformly withheld, because a page that could not show or collect any would have no way to register a server at all. **(1) Input the person authors in the declaration form**: transport, command, arguments, URL, timeouts, the required-server marker, and environment-variable and header **names**. **(2) Read-back**, which is exactly the daemon's redacted config view and nothing beyond it — the same non-secret fields, with secret **values** structurally absent — so the page can support an edit workflow without the daemon ever serving credential material ([Spec-024 §Unified Inventory](024-mcp-server-configuration-and-governance.md#unified-inventory)). **(3) Never rendered**: any value the daemon does not serve — environment-variable values, header values, tokens, URL query values — and the page reads no provider config file, which remains the source of truth and the daemon's to read. Credential-bearing values the form does collect are **write-only in the renderer as well as on the wire**, on the Provider Accounts token-field discipline above: masked on entry, never read back, never held in serializable renderer state a devtools inspection or crash report would capture, and cleared on submit. The two governance **event** payloads remain credential-free and content-free by construction, and the page adds nothing to them
- Interactions: add or update a server declaration, remove one, enable or disable one, set or clear a per-tool idempotency-class override, sign in to a server, sign out of one, search the public MCP Registry, and reconnect — each forwarded to the daemon as **one wire mutation per explicit user action**, the same discipline the Provider Accounts view states: nothing batches, nothing retries on the person's behalf, and nothing hides an act the daemon would refuse. Reconnect is offered on the selected server whatever its state and drawn first on one reading failed; it asks the provider to open the connection again, writes nothing and changes no setting, says so, and where it moves no state it says that nothing changed rather than reading as success. `Sign in to this server` is one control whatever the server admits, and gives the background service one OAuth sign-in for that server, used by both providers: by default the service signs in as its own client; a server whose entry names a client its owner issued is signed in as that client; a server whose authorization server admits only Claude Code's or Codex's own client is signed in through the provider it admits, the service then holding that sign-in as its own; and a server that demands proof-of-possession (DPoP) tokens, which neither provider can sign in to, is signed in by the service with a signing key made for that one sign-in and fronted by the service for both providers, which signs a fresh proof for every request. The control stays pressable while a sign-in waits, a press ending that wait and starting the next attempt, and the page renders the daemon-returned authorization URL and settles on the daemon's own completion event rather than on a timer or on the browser returning — the same shape a brokered provider sign-in already takes on the Provider Accounts page. The service keeps each refresh token in the operating system's credential store under its own item and renews it itself, and each provider process it launches gets a header helper that asks the service for a current access token, so no token is logged, relayed, shown or given to the renderer ([Spec-024 §OAuth Orchestration](024-mcp-server-configuration-and-governance.md#oauth-orchestration)). That URL is **transient render state and nothing else**: it arrives on the login reply alone, is held only for the life of the attempt, is written to no renderer storage and no log, and is dropped at settlement — the write-only discipline this page already applies to a credential-bearing form value, applied to a value that is a one-shot launch handle rather than a credential. It is admitted to the renderer as the daemon's own reply value rather than as renderer-composed input, which is the distinction the external-open path exists to police. The prohibition it does not touch is the one that matters: tokens, authorization codes, and PKCE material reach this page on no surface at all. Once a server is signed in, `Sign out of this server` stands where `Sign in to this server` stood and forgets the sign-in at once (`mcp.oauthLogout`), the server then reading `Needs sign-in`. `Browse servers`, beside `Add a server`, opens a search over the public MCP Registry (`mcp.registrySearch`) under the line `Searches the public MCP Registry. Anyone can publish there; check a server's source before adding it.`, searching only when the person types; each result reads its title or name, its description, its version, and whether it runs as a package or is reached at an address, and picking one opens `Add a server` filled in with its name, how it runs, and each environment variable's name and description, the required ones marked. A refusal renders the daemon's typed reason verbatim — a refused action is refused before any write, and the page shows that refusal rather than pre-filtering the control, so the person learns the rule from the system that enforces it
- State handling: every rendered value is wire-verbatim, and **the page derives no state of its own** — connection status, enablement, tool overrides, and effective idempotency class are all rendered from what the daemon reports, the fail-closed-projection discipline `I-002-18` states for the edit affordance. A page that recomputed effective class would be a second source of truth for exactly the decisions [Spec-013](013-persistence-and-recovery.md) recovery depends on. **Eligibility is a separate question and this page does not project it at all**: no inventory field reports whether an operation would be permitted, so every control is offered, the daemon adjudicates, and its typed refusal renders — which is the same rule as the no-pre-filtering interaction above rather than a second one. The one non-offer is a control whose required input is **structurally absent** from the entry the daemon served — on the degraded arm the daemon serves when the binding store is unreachable, the per-tool overrides are absent outright and the enabled marker survives only where the provider itself declares it, so the per-tool controls have nothing to act on while every control whose input did arrive is offered as usual — and that is absence of data, not a judgment the renderer formed. Status arriving on the subscription is applied to the row it names rather than triggering a whole-inventory re-read
- Owning plan: Plan-022
- Note: a settings page, not a V1 Signature Feature — it composes through the same preload bridge under the same read-and-steer rule.

## Pitfalls To Avoid

- **Giving the renderer any form of Node or process access.** `nodeIntegration: true` or `sandbox: false` in any window must be treated as a build-time error.
- **Leaking auth material across the bridge.** Audit the preload surface — any function returning a PASETO token (including the relay `connectionToken`), DPoP proof, or daemon session token to the renderer is a security regression. Relay negotiation runs in the background service; its `connectionToken` never reaches main and MUST NOT appear in any bridge response.
- **Skipping asar integrity verification in release builds.** `EnableEmbeddedAsarIntegrityValidation` must be enabled for every release; disabling it (even temporarily for debugging) in a signed release binary defeats the tamper-detection posture.
- **Coupling renderer lifecycle to daemon lifecycle.** Daemon disconnect must leave the renderer as it was — the draft keeping its text, the panes open, no control disabled — and never crash it. Daemon crash with automatic restart must not lose renderer state.
- **Letting the renderer hold session-scoped state that should be re-derived from the daemon on reconnect.** State kept for fast UX (e.g., scroll position) is fine; state kept as truth (e.g., pending-approval list) creates divergence when the daemon reconnects.
- **Registering the `sidekicks://` protocol handler without conflict resolution.** On first run, if a prior installation already claims the handler, the new installation must either displace it explicitly or surface the conflict.
- **Rotating the macOS code-signing identity as if it were an ordinary secret.** An update installs only when it satisfies the running app's designated requirement, which names that identity's certificate ([Desktop App Implementation Notes §Code Signing And Notarization](../architecture/desktop-implementation-notes.md#code-signing-and-notarization)), so a rotation mishap stops every installed copy from updating.
- **Shipping renderer code that imports Node built-ins.** Vite's build must fail fast on accidental Node imports in the renderer bundle.
- **Polling the daemon on an interval from the renderer.** Reads happen on subscribe, on window focus, on reconnect, and on the terminal events the owning spec names; a renderer timer that re-reads on a cadence is the idle-CPU budget's first casualty and a second source of freshness the subscription already provides (`§Console Design (Meridian)`).
- **Projecting eligibility in the renderer.** A control the daemon may refuse is offered and its typed refusal is rendered; a renderer that pre-denies re-derives a rule it does not own, the `I-002-18` fail-closed-projection discipline every console surface generalizes.
- **Leaving fixture scenarios in a release bundle.** Fixture code and data — every scenario and each fixture beside the boundary it substitutes — sit behind the same compile-time `define` gate as the smoke probes in the next bullet, so a release build carries no fixture code, no catalog and no loader, and `--fixture` there is a startup error; a runtime `process.env` gate around any of them is rejected, not a style choice.
- **Embedding test-only code paths in production binaries.** Test-machinery (smoke probes, debug-only branches, internal instrumentation hooks) must be eliminated from release bundles via a compile-time-static gate — e.g., a Vite `define`-substituted identifier (`__SMOKE_BUILD__`-style) so Rollup's tree-shaker collapses `if (false && expr)` branches and physically strips the probe body from the shipped artifact. A runtime-only env-var check (`if (process.env.SOME_FLAG === '1')`) leaves the test code present in the bundle and weakens the §Trust Stance posture + earlier bullets in this section (every additional code path in a release binary is reachable surface). The runtime check is fine as defense-in-depth on the inner side of the compile-time gate, but never as the sole gate.

## Acceptance Criteria

- [ ] The main process creates every window as a `BaseWindow` and every console document it shows as a `WebContentsView` with `contextIsolation: true`, `sandbox: true`, `nodeIntegration: false` and `webSecurity: true`; the hidden console document carries the one preload script, which uses only `contextBridge.exposeInMainWorld`; every window a person sees, the first included — a window of session views or a pane's own window — is the hidden console document's `window.open` child, adopted in a `WebContentsView`, keeping the renderer's sandbox, isolation and content security policy and carrying no preload; every other `window.open` is denied; and no code path constructs a `BrowserWindow` — verified by contract test
- [ ] No auth material (daemon session token, PASETO tokens, DPoP key) appears on the `window.desktopBridge` surface — verified by a negative contract test against the desktop's preload API type
- [ ] Every Electron Fuse declared in §Security Hardening Baseline is set to the declared value in every released binary — verified by a build-time assertion
- [ ] Main starts the daemon detached (`child_process.spawn` with `detached`), never as a child of the app and never through `utilityProcess.fork`, reaches it through its socket, restarts it on a crash with the declared backoff, and surfaces the persistent-error state after five failed attempts — verified by integration test
- [ ] A quit flushes the service (`daemon.flush`), exiting when the flush answers or after ten seconds, and leaves the service, every run and every terminal shell running: on macOS and Linux, after the app quits and after its process is sent SIGTERM or SIGKILL, the service still answers `daemon.hello` on its socket and a run started before the quit is still running; only Settings › Runtime's `Stop` and `Restart` end work — verified by integration test
- [ ] The update path is exercised end to end on every release platform in CI: a release whose version is not above the last one seen is refused; a downloaded file whose SHA-512 does not match the release's update file, or on Windows whose publisher does not match, is discarded; `Restart to finish` installs only a checked file; and after the restart the daemon handshake succeeds — verified by Plan-020 release-gate test
- [ ] Release artifacts are signed on every platform and pass its check: every macOS build carries the project's one self-signed identity and an update signed with it installs over the build before it, with notarization and Gatekeeper's pass once the Apple Developer ID certificate exists; on Windows 10 and 11, once SignPath Foundation's signing is in place, the NSIS installer and `sidekicks-windows-half.exe` carry its signature and pass SmartScreen's signed-file check; and on Linux the apt and dnf repositories' signatures verify on install and on update — verified by Plan-020 release smoke test
- [ ] Every Signature Feature view (the transcript, approvals, runs, the agents pane) composes the owning-plan contract without duplicating daemon logic — verified by renderer unit tests against mocked bridge methods
- [ ] The app's bundle size (post-asar, post-compression) is under the ADR-015 Success Criteria target of 150 MB — verified in CI by the `size-limit` check that measures each packaged installer with compression off
- [ ] The preload bridge surface has no `any`-typed escape hatch in its public type — verified by TypeScript strict-mode build
- [ ] Renderer attempts to access `require`, `process`, or `global` return `undefined` — verified by runtime assertion in a sandbox test
- [ ] Every window in main's registry of windows remains V8-reachable across the `app.whenReady().then(...)` callback unwind in a release-mode build, such that no window is collected while it is open — `window-all-closed` never fires, because the hidden console window stays until quit, and main counts the windows a person sees for the last-window behavior — verified by a `--js-flags=--expose-gc` lifecycle probe asserting (a) a `v8.queryObjects(BaseWindow)` count that holds stable across repeated GC pressure cycles and drops by at least one per window once every window a person sees is closed and the close has unwound — a per-window delta, so the fixed non-instance match a count-only sample cannot identify is never mistaken for the instance — AND (b) `window-all-closed` does not fire during the probe iteration loop. Per [ADR-023 §Antithesis — The Strongest Case Against](../decisions/023-electron-main-process-window-retention.md#antithesis--the-strongest-case-against), the load-bearing reachability mechanism is Electron's native-side `BaseWindow::self_ref_` (the `v8::Global<v8::Value>` field declared in `shell/browser/api/electron_api_base_window.h`, set in `BaseWindow::InitWith`, and reset only in `~BaseWindow` at native-object destruction — tag-exact lines in [ADR-023 §Primary sources (Electron v41.6.1)](../decisions/023-electron-main-process-window-retention.md#primary-sources-electron-v4161)); main's module-scope registry is defensive consistency with the canonical Electron community pattern, not the primary GC-anchor.
- [ ] Every message the person sent carries Copy, Fork, Edit and `Undo to here` on both providers and through a running turn, and is never without them; the pencil's resend and `Undo to here` go through the daemon's `session.restorePreview` and `session.restore`: the dry run's count shows before the undo asks, the turns after that point dim while a resend is in flight and stay in the flow marked superseded once it lands, a resend that fails puts the edited message back in the composer and leaves the conversation as it was, and an undo that applied only in part names what came back and what did not with its cause — every one of these rendered from the daemon's result and never re-derived in the renderer — verified by renderer unit tests against a mocked bridge
- [ ] The composer's command list offers only the entries enumerated under the session's own `(driverName, providerAccountId)` binding, and a change of that binding re-reads the enumeration rather than filtering the old one — a command enumerated on one provider is never listed for, or dispatched through, a session bound to the other.
- [ ] Each capability-gated composer affordance is **absent** rather than present-and-disabled where its driver flag is `false` — the compaction popover, the permission levels the level control lists and the rungs the effort control offers — with the one named exception §Rules every console surface obeys carries: the output-speed control, drawn on every session and grayed in place where the chosen model has no fast output, its reason both in its own hover title and in one small faint line beside the composer's chips naming that model. The compaction popover's completed state renders only on the daemon's compaction row rather than on the call returning.
- [ ] The MCP Servers page renders every value wire-verbatim from the `mcp.*` reads and the governance subscription, recomputes no effective idempotency class, renders no **withheld** configuration value — no environment-variable value, header value, URL query value, or token — while still rendering the daemon's redacted config read-back so the declaration can be inspected and edited, holds an OAuth launch URL as transient state that is persisted nowhere and dropped at settlement, and issues exactly one wire mutation per explicit act by the person.
- [ ] Every console surface renders in the Meridian design language of `§Console Design (Meridian)`: an agent's identity is carried by its hue, the person carries no identity mark, and attention is carried by amber and red alone; every wire figure renders in the mono face verbatim, and the two-hue rule and the contrast floors rule 3 of §Meridian, the design language sets are enforced at the token level — verified by the accessibility sweep over every route and dialog and by review of the rendered screen
- [ ] The console builds and its renderer tests run against fixtures, each beside the boundary it substitutes and answering only that boundary's operations; `--fixture <scenario> [--session <session-id>]` starts one of the fixture scenarios, and an unknown scenario, a missing value or a session the scenario does not hold stops startup with an error that names it; and a release bundle carries no fixture code, no catalog and no loader, so `--fixture` there is a startup error — verified by the release fixture gate (the release build's own source maps against the one fixture predicate, the imported-constant sweeps and the stylesheet dependency rule) and by a launch test that starts a release build with `--fixture transcript-states` and sees it exit with the startup error
- [ ] The budgets in `§Console Design (Meridian)` fail CI on a regression: the renderer initial bundle ≤ 450 kB gzip on every console PR; and, once the macOS build is complete and on every PR from then on, idle CPU ≤ 0.5 % of one core with a session open, renderer heap ≤ 120 MB with one session open at rest, and p95 frame time ≤ 16.7 ms with four lanes streaming — verified by the bundle gate and the endurance ledger
- [ ] `Mute notifications` in a session's actions list (`session.mute`) silences that session's `Finished` and `Failed` notifications on every channel and every device and never its `Waiting on you` or a workflow's Notify step, and `Unmute notifications` (`session.unmute`) undoes it; the switches `Waiting on you`, `Finished`, `Failed` and `Notify steps` under `Notify me outside the app` are kept per device in the machine's settings file, each stops only its own kind's banner on this machine, and the bell's list and the icon count are unchanged by either — verified by renderer unit tests against a mocked bridge
- [ ] Every window satisfies the first criterion in this list and is driven by the one renderer: every window a person sees, the first included, is a `window.open` child of the hidden console document main builds at start and never shows, answered by main's window-open handler with its own `BaseWindow` built from main's own options, carrying no preload, no bridge of their own and no auth material; main pairs the closes of the window and its child both ways; closing or minimizing any window a person sees leaves every other one drawing; main counts the windows a person sees for the last-window behavior; and no console window is ever in Preview's relay target list — verified by the multi-window end-to-end tier
- [ ] Any side pane — Review, Preview, the terminal, the file pane, the inspector and the agents pane — moves into a window of its own with `⌘⇧A` or the pane header's `Open in new window`, one window per pane per session, and the act on a pane already in its own window brings that window forward; `Return to session`, `⌘⇧A` pressed in that window and closing it each return the pane to its session's view, or open the session in the window used last where it is on screen nowhere; the pane's window belongs to its session and stays when the window it came from closes; a Preview page moves whole with its pane, with no reload, the agent's session and the screencast carrying on; and a pane in its own window at quit comes back in its own window — verified by the multi-window end-to-end tier
- [ ] A window holds one or more session views side by side; each session shows in one view on this device and each destination in one window, and asking for either again, by any route, brings its place forward; a session's address, `sidekicks open`, a notification click and a bell line bring forward the view that shows the session, or else switch the focused view of the window used last to it, or open a window on it when none is open; a window's title is its focused view's `<session title> — <project>`, and a pane's own window's `<Pane> — <session title>`; every window, its views with their order, widths and focus, and every pane's own window come back after a restart and after a renderer crash, unless the crashes repeat ([Desktop App Implementation Notes §Repeated renderer crashes](../architecture/desktop-implementation-notes.md#repeated-renderer-crashes)); closing a view or every window stops no session; and the memory and processor cost of four windows showing eight sessions is measured against one window showing one — verified by the multi-window end-to-end tier
- [ ] The menu bar carries About on every platform — in the macOS app menu beside Hide, Hide Others, Show All and Quit, and in the Help menu on Windows and Linux — showing the app icon, its name and the running version through the platform's own panel, or Electron's message box on Windows; the macOS menu-bar icon shows needs you whenever the attention control's count is above zero, working whenever nothing waits and a session or workflow run on this machine is running, and idle otherwise, swapping one template image for another and never animating.
- [ ] No launch or splash screen comes before the first console window; a session view holding no session draws the logo mark, hidden from assistive technology, and drops it the moment a session opens in the view, and no other surface draws it.
- [ ] No debug port is open on either host: neither `--remote-debugging-port` nor `--remote-debugging-pipe` is set; the agent's browser tools reach desktop pages only through the daemon's relay over Electron's in-process debugger, whose target list is exactly the open Preview pages with every console window absent; and on a page's session every `Target.*` and `Browser.*` command outside the page-session filter is refused — verified by the Electron tier's end-to-end test

## Open Questions

- **Windows 10 support horizon:** Windows 10 reached end of support on 2025-10-14. V1 supports Windows 10 and 11, x64 and arm64. Whether a later release supports Windows 11 only is a scope decision for the project's owner, not a technical blocker.
